    50→class ScanStateContractError(ValueError):
    51→    """Raised when persisted scan state violates required runtime contracts."""
    52→
    53→
    54→def _state_subjective_assessments(
    55→    state: state_mod.StateModel,
    56→) -> dict[str, object]:
    57→    """Return normalized subjective assessment store from state."""
    58→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    59→    if isinstance(assessments, dict):
    60→        return assessments
    61→    raise ScanStateContractError(
    62→        "state.subjective_assessments must be an object; rerun with a valid state file"
    63→    )
    64→
    65→
    66→def _state_lang_capabilities(
    67→    state: state_mod.StateModel,
    68→) -> dict[str, dict[str, object]]:
    69→    """Return normalized language capability map from state."""
    70→    capabilities = state.get("lang_capabilities")
    71→    if capabilities is None:
    72→        normalized: dict[str, dict[str, object]] = {}
    73→        state["lang_capabilities"] = normalized
    74→        return normalized
    75→    if isinstance(capabilities, dict):
    76→        return capabilities
    77→    raise ScanStateContractError(
    78→        "state.lang_capabilities must be an object when present"
    79→    )
    80→
    81→
    82→def _state_findings(state: state_mod.StateModel) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:
    83→    """Return normalized finding map from state."""
    84→    findings = state.get("findings")
    85→    if isinstance(findings, dict):
    86→        return findings
    87→    raise ScanStateContractError(
    88→        "state.findings must be an object; state file appears corrupted"
    89→    )

   100→
   101→@dataclass
   102→class ScanRuntime:
   103→    """Resolved runtime context for a single scan invocation."""
   104→
   105→    args: argparse.Namespace
   106→    state_path: Path | None
   107→    state: state_mod.StateModel
   108→    path: Path
   109→    config: dict[str, object]
   110→    lang: LangRun | None
   111→    lang_label: str
   112→    profile: str
   113→    effective_include_slow: bool
   114→    zone_overrides: dict[str, object] | None
   115→    reset_subjective_count: int = 0
   116→    expired_manual_override_count: int = 0
   117→    coverage_warnings: list[DetectorCoverageRecord] = field(default_factory=list)
   118→
   119→
   120→@dataclass
   121→class ScanMergeResult:
   122→    """State merge outputs and previous score snapshots."""
   123→
   124→    diff: dict[str, object]
   125→    prev_overall: float | None
   126→    prev_objective: float | None
   127→    prev_strict: float | None
   128→    prev_verified: float | None
   129→    prev_dim_scores: dict[str, object]
   130→
   131→
   132→@dataclass
   133→class ScanNoiseSnapshot:
   134→    """Noise budget settings and hidden finding counts for this scan."""
   135→
   136→    noise_budget: int
   137→    global_noise_budget: int
   138→    budget_warning: str | None
   139→    hidden_by_detector: dict[str, int]
   140→    hidden_total: int
   141→
   142→
   143→def _configure_lang_runtime(
   144→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   145→    config: dict[str, object],
   146→    state: state_mod.StateModel,
   147→    lang: LangRun | None,
   148→) -> LangRun | None:
   149→    """Populate runtime context and threshold overrides for a selected language."""
   150→    if not lang:
   151→        return None
   152→
   153→    lang_options = resolve_lang_runtime_options(args, lang)
   154→    lang_settings = resolve_lang_settings(config, lang)
   155→    runtime_lang = make_lang_run(
   156→        lang,
   157→        overrides=LangRunOverrides(
   158→            review_cache=state.get("review_cache", {}),
   159→            review_max_age_days=config.get("review_max_age_days", 30),
   160→            runtime_settings=lang_settings,
   161→            runtime_options=lang_options,
   162→            large_threshold_override=config.get("large_files_threshold", 0),
   163→            props_threshold_override=config.get("props_threshold", 0),
   164→        ),
   165→    )
   166→
   167→    lang_capabilities = _state_lang_capabilities(state)
   168→    lang_capabilities[runtime_lang.name] = {
   169→        "fixers": sorted(runtime_lang.fixers.keys()),
   170→        "typecheck_cmd": runtime_lang.typecheck_cmd,
   171→    }
   172→    return runtime_lang
   173→
   174→
   175→def _reset_subjective_assessments_for_scan_reset(
   176→    state: state_mod.StateModel,
   177→    *,
   178→    lang_name: str | None = None,
   179→) -> int:
   180→    """Reset known subjective dimensions to 0 so the next scan starts fresh."""
   181→    assessments = _state_subjective_assessments(state)
   182→
   183→    reset_keys = {
   184→        key.strip()
   185→        for key in assessments
   186→        if isinstance(key, str) and key.strip()
   187→    }
   188→    reset_keys.update(_subjective_reset_dimensions(lang_name=lang_name))
   189→
   190→    now = state_mod.utc_now()
   191→    for key in sorted(reset_keys):
   192→        payload = assessments.get(key)
   193→        if isinstance(payload, dict):
   194→            payload["score"] = 0.0
   195→            payload["source"] = "scan_reset_subjective"
   196→            payload["assessed_at"] = now
   197→            payload["reset_by"] = "scan_reset_subjective"
   198→            payload["placeholder"] = True
   199→            payload.pop("integrity_penalty", None)
   200→            payload.pop("components", None)
   201→            payload.pop("component_scores", None)
   202→            continue
   203→        assessments[key] = {
   204→            "score": 0.0,
   205→            "source": "scan_reset_subjective",
   206→            "assessed_at": now,
   207→            "reset_by": "scan_reset_subjective",
   208→            "placeholder": True,
   209→        }
   210→    return len(reset_keys)
   211→
   212→
   213→def _expire_provisional_manual_override_assessments(
   214→    state: state_mod.StateModel,
   215→) -> int:
   216→    """Expire provisional manual-override assessments at scan start."""
   217→    assessments = _state_subjective_assessments(state)
   218→
   219→    now = state_mod.utc_now()
   220→    expired = 0
   221→    for payload in assessments.values():
   222→        if not isinstance(payload, dict):
   223→            continue
   224→        if payload.get("provisional_override") is not True:
   225→            continue
   226→        payload["score"] = 0.0
   227→        payload["source"] = "manual_override_expired"
   228→        payload["assessed_at"] = now
   229→        payload["reset_by"] = "manual_override_expired"
   230→        payload["placeholder"] = True
   231→        payload.pop("provisional_override", None)
   232→        payload.pop("provisional_until_scan", None)
   233→        payload.pop("integrity_penalty", None)
   234→        payload.pop("components", None)
   235→        payload.pop("component_scores", None)
   236→        expired += 1
   237→    return expired
   238→
   239→
   240→def prepare_scan_runtime(args) -> ScanRuntime:
   241→    """Resolve state/config/language and apply scan-time runtime settings."""
   242→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   243→    state_file = runtime.state_path
   244→    state = runtime.state if isinstance(runtime.state, dict) else {}
   245→    state_mod.ensure_state_defaults(state)
   246→    path = Path(args.path)
   247→    config = runtime.config if isinstance(runtime.config, dict) else {}
   248→    lang_config = resolve_lang(args)
   249→    reset_subjective_count = 0
   250→    expired_manual_override_count = _expire_provisional_manual_override_assessments(
   251→        state
   252→    )
   253→    if getattr(args, "reset_subjective", False):
   254→        reset_subjective_count = _reset_subjective_assessments_for_scan_reset(
   255→            state,
   256→            lang_name=getattr(lang_config, "name", None),
   257→        )
   258→
   259→    include_slow = not getattr(args, "skip_slow", False)
   260→    profile = _resolve_scan_profile(getattr(args, "profile", None), lang_config)
   261→    effective_include_slow = _effective_include_slow(include_slow, profile)
   262→
   263→    lang = _configure_lang_runtime(args, config, state, lang_config)
   264→    coverage_warnings = _seed_runtime_coverage_warnings(lang)
   265→    zone_overrides_raw = config.get("zone_overrides")
   266→    zone_overrides = zone_overrides_raw if isinstance(zone_overrides_raw, dict) else None
   267→
   268→    return ScanRuntime(
   269→        args=args,
   270→        state_path=state_file,
   271→        state=state,
   272→        path=path,
   273→        config=config,
   274→        lang=lang,
   275→        lang_label=f" ({lang.name})" if lang else "",
   276→        profile=profile,
   277→        effective_include_slow=effective_include_slow,
   278→        zone_overrides=zone_overrides,
   279→        reset_subjective_count=reset_subjective_count,
   280→        expired_manual_override_count=expired_manual_override_count,
   281→        coverage_warnings=coverage_warnings,
   282→    )
   283→
   284→
   285→def _augment_with_stale_exclusion_findings(
   286→    findings: list[dict[str, Any]],
   287→    runtime: ScanRuntime,
   288→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   289→    """Append stale exclude findings when excluded dirs are unreferenced."""
   290→    extra_exclusions = get_exclusions()
   291→    if not (extra_exclusions and runtime.lang and runtime.lang.file_finder):
   292→        return findings
   293→
   294→    scanned_files = runtime.lang.file_finder(runtime.path)
   295→    stale = _audit_excluded_dirs(
   296→        extra_exclusions, scanned_files, PROJECT_ROOT
   297→    )
   298→    if not stale:
   299→        return findings
   300→
   301→    augmented = list(findings)
   302→    augmented.extend(stale)
   303→    for stale_finding in stale:
   304→        print(colorize(f"  ℹ {stale_finding['summary']}", "dim"))
   305→    return augmented
   306→
   307→
   308→def _augment_with_stale_wontfix_findings(
   309→    findings: list[dict[str, Any]],
   310→    runtime: ScanRuntime,
   311→    *,
   312→    decay_scans: int,
   313→) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], int]:
   314→    """Append re-triage findings for stale/worsening wontfix debt."""
   315→    return _augment_stale_wontfix_impl(
   316→        findings,
   317→        state=runtime.state,
   318→        scan_path=runtime.path,
   319→        project_root=PROJECT_ROOT,
   320→        decay_scans=decay_scans,
   321→    )
   322→
   323→
   324→def run_scan_generation(
   325→    runtime: ScanRuntime,
   326→) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], dict[str, object], dict[str, object] | None]:
   327→    """Run detector pipeline and return findings, potentials, and codebase metrics."""
   328→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import (
   329→        disable_parse_cache,
   330→        enable_parse_cache,
   331→    )
   332→
   333→    enable_file_cache()
   334→    enable_parse_cache()
   335→    try:
   336→        findings, potentials = plan_mod.generate_findings(
   337→            runtime.path,
   338→            lang=runtime.lang,
   339→            options=PlanScanOptions(
   340→                include_slow=runtime.effective_include_slow,
   341→                zone_overrides=runtime.zone_overrides,
   342→                profile=runtime.profile,
   343→            ),
   344→        )
   345→    finally:
   346→        disable_parse_cache()
   347→        disable_file_cache()
   348→
   349→    codebase_metrics = _collect_codebase_metrics(runtime.lang, runtime.path)
   350→    _warn_explicit_lang_with_no_files(
   351→        runtime.args, runtime.lang, runtime.path, codebase_metrics
   352→    )
   353→    findings = _augment_with_stale_exclusion_findings(findings, runtime)
   354→    decay_scans = _coerce_int(
   355→        runtime.config.get("wontfix_decay_scans"),
   356→        default=_WONTFIX_DECAY_SCANS_DEFAULT,
   357→    )
   358→    findings, monitored_wontfix = _augment_with_stale_wontfix_findings(
   359→        findings,
   360→        runtime,
   361→        decay_scans=max(decay_scans, 0),
   362→    )
   363→    potentials["stale_wontfix"] = monitored_wontfix
   364→    return findings, potentials, codebase_metrics
   365→
   366→
   367→def merge_scan_results(
   368→    runtime: ScanRuntime,
   369→    findings: list[dict[str, Any]],
   370→    potentials: dict[str, object],
   371→    codebase_metrics: dict[str, object] | None,
   372→) -> ScanMergeResult:
   373→    """Merge findings into persistent state and return diff + previous score snapshot."""
   374→    scan_path_rel = rel(str(runtime.path))
   375→    prev_scan_path = runtime.state.get("scan_path")
   376→    path_changed = prev_scan_path is not None and prev_scan_path != scan_path_rel
   377→
   378→    if not path_changed:
   379→        prev = state_mod.score_snapshot(runtime.state)
   380→    else:
   381→        prev = state_mod.ScoreSnapshot(None, None, None, None)
   382→    prev_dim_scores = (
   383→        runtime.state.get("dimension_scores", {}) if not path_changed else {}
   384→    )
   385→
   386→    if runtime.lang and runtime.lang.zone_map is not None:
   387→        runtime.state["zone_distribution"] = runtime.lang.zone_map.counts()
   388→    _persist_scan_coverage(runtime.state, runtime.lang)
   389→
   390→    target_score = target_strict_score_from_config(runtime.config, fallback=95.0)
   391→
   392→    diff = state_mod.merge_scan(
   393→        runtime.state,
   394→        findings,
   395→        options=state_mod.MergeScanOptions(
   396→            lang=runtime.lang.name if runtime.lang else None,
   397→            scan_path=scan_path_rel,
   398→            force_resolve=getattr(runtime.args, "force_resolve", False),
   399→            exclude=get_exclusions(),
   400→            potentials=potentials,
   401→            codebase_metrics=codebase_metrics,
   402→            include_slow=runtime.effective_include_slow,
   403→            ignore=runtime.config.get("ignore", []),
   404→            subjective_integrity_target=target_score,
   405→        ),
   406→    )
   407→
   408→    issues_mod.expire_stale_holistic(
   409→        runtime.state, runtime.config.get("holistic_max_age_days", 30)
   410→    )
   411→    state_mod.save_state(
   412→        runtime.state,
   413→        runtime.state_path,
   414→        subjective_integrity_target=target_score,
   415→    )
   416→
   417→    # Reconcile living plan + sync stale subjective dimensions
   418→    try:
   419→        from desloppify.engine.plan import (
   420→            load_plan,
   421→            reconcile_plan_after_scan,
   422→            save_plan,
   423→            sync_stale_dimensions,
   424→        )
   425→
   426→        plan_path = runtime.state_path.parent / "plan.json"
   427→        plan = load_plan(plan_path)
   428→        dirty = False
   429→
   430→        # Standard reconciliation (only if plan has user content)
   431→        if (
   432→            plan.get("queue_order")
   433→            or plan.get("overrides")
   434→            or plan.get("clusters")
   435→            or plan.get("skipped")
   436→        ):
   437→            recon = reconcile_plan_after_scan(plan, runtime.state)
   438→            if recon.changes:
   439→                dirty = True
   440→            if recon.resurfaced:
   441→                print(colorize(
   442→                    f"  Plan: {len(recon.resurfaced)} skipped item(s) re-surfaced after review period.",
   443→                    "cyan",
   444→                ))
   445→
   446→        # Stale dimension sync (always — handles both cleanup and injection)
   447→        dim_sync = sync_stale_dimensions(plan, runtime.state)
   448→        if dim_sync.changes:
   449→            dirty = True
   450→        if dim_sync.pruned:
   451→            print(colorize(
   452→                f"  Plan: {len(dim_sync.pruned)} refreshed subjective dimension(s) removed from queue.",
   453→                "cyan",
   454→            ))
   455→        if dim_sync.injected:
   456→            print(colorize(
   457→                f"  Plan: {len(dim_sync.injected)} stale subjective dimension(s) queued for refresh.",
   458→                "cyan",
   459→            ))
   460→
   461→        # Auto-cluster findings into task groups
   462→        from desloppify.engine._plan.auto_cluster import auto_cluster_findings
   463→
   464→        ac_changes = auto_cluster_findings(plan, runtime.state)
   465→        if ac_changes:
   466→            dirty = True
   467→
   468→        if dirty:
   469→            save_plan(plan, plan_path)
   470→    except Exception:
   471→        pass  # Plan reconciliation is best-effort
   472→
   473→    return ScanMergeResult(
   474→        diff=diff,
   475→        prev_overall=prev.overall,
   476→        prev_objective=prev.objective,
   477→        prev_strict=prev.strict,
   478→        prev_verified=prev.verified,
   479→        prev_dim_scores=prev_dim_scores,
   480→    )
   481→
   482→
   483→def resolve_noise_snapshot(
   484→    state: state_mod.StateModel,
   485→    config: dict[str, object],
   486→) -> ScanNoiseSnapshot:
   487→    """Resolve noise budget settings and hidden finding counters."""
   488→    noise_budget, global_noise_budget, budget_warning = (
   489→        state_mod.resolve_finding_noise_settings(config)
   490→    )
   491→    findings_by_id = _state_findings(state)
   492→    open_findings = [
   493→        finding
   494→        for finding in state_mod.path_scoped_findings(
   495→            findings_by_id, state.get("scan_path")
   496→        ).values()
   497→        if finding.get("status") == "open"
   498→    ]
   499→    _, hidden_by_detector = state_mod.apply_finding_noise_budget(
   500→        open_findings,
   501→        budget=noise_budget,
   502→        global_budget=global_noise_budget,
   503→    )
   504→
   505→    return ScanNoiseSnapshot(
   506→        noise_budget=noise_budget,
   507→        global_noise_budget=global_noise_budget,
   508→        budget_warning=budget_warning,
   509→        hidden_by_detector=hidden_by_detector,
   510→        hidden_total=sum(hidden_by_detector.values()),
   511→    )
   512→
   513→
   514→def persist_reminder_history(
   515→    runtime: ScanRuntime,
   516→    narrative: dict[str, object],
   517→) -> None:
   518→    """Persist reminder history emitted by narrative computation."""
   519→    if not (narrative and "reminder_history" in narrative):
   520→        return
   521→
   522→    runtime.state["reminder_history"] = narrative["reminder_history"]
   523→    target_score = target_strict_score_from_config(runtime.config, fallback=95.0)
   524→    state_mod.save_state(
   525→        runtime.state,
   526→        runtime.state_path,
   527→        subjective_integrity_target=target_score,
   528→    )
   529→
   530→
   531→__all__ = [
   532→    "ScanStateContractError",
   533→    "ScanMergeResult",
   534→    "ScanNoiseSnapshot",
   535→    "ScanRuntime",
   536→    "merge_scan_results",
   537→    "persist_reminder_history",
   538→    "prepare_scan_runtime",
   539→    "resolve_noise_snapshot",
   540→    "run_scan_generation",
   541→]
   542→

    34→# Mechanical detectors → subjective dimensions they provide evidence for.
    35→# When findings from these detectors change during a scan, the corresponding
    36→# subjective assessments are marked stale so reviewers know to re-evaluate.
    37→_DETECTOR_SUBJECTIVE_DIMENSIONS: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {
    38→    "structural": ("design_coherence", "abstraction_fitness"),
    39→    "smells": ("design_coherence", "error_consistency"),
    40→    "global_mutable_config": ("initialization_coupling",),
    41→    "coupling": ("cross_module_architecture",),
    42→    "layer_violation": ("cross_module_architecture",),
    43→    "private_imports": ("cross_module_architecture",),
    44→    "dupes": ("convention_outlier",),
    45→    "boilerplate_duplication": ("convention_outlier",),
    46→    "naming": ("convention_outlier",),
    47→    "flat_dirs": ("package_organization",),
    48→    "orphaned": ("design_coherence",),
    49→    "uncalled_functions": ("design_coherence",),
    50→    "responsibility_cohesion": ("design_coherence", "abstraction_fitness"),
    51→    "cycles": ("cross_module_architecture", "dependency_health"),
    52→}
    53→
    54→
    55→def _mark_stale_on_mechanical_change(
    56→    state: StateModel,
    57→    *,
    58→    changed_detectors: set[str],
    59→    now: str,
    60→) -> None:
    61→    """Mark subjective assessments stale when mechanical findings change.
    62→
    63→    Only marks dimensions that already have an assessment — doesn't create
    64→    new entries for dimensions that have never been reviewed.
    65→    """
    66→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    67→    if not isinstance(assessments, dict) or not assessments:
    68→        return
    69→
    70→    affected_dims: set[str] = set()
    71→    for detector in changed_detectors:
    72→        dims = _DETECTOR_SUBJECTIVE_DIMENSIONS.get(detector, ())
    73→        affected_dims.update(dims)
    74→
    75→    if not affected_dims:
    76→        return
    77→
    78→    for dimension in sorted(affected_dims):
    79→        if dimension not in assessments:
    80→            continue
    81→        payload = assessments[dimension]
    82→        if not isinstance(payload, dict):
    83→            continue
    84→        # Don't overwrite if already stale
    85→        if payload.get("needs_review_refresh"):
    86→            continue
    87→        payload["needs_review_refresh"] = True
    88→        payload["refresh_reason"] = "mechanical_findings_changed"
    89→        payload["stale_since"] = now
    90→
    91→
    92→@dataclass
    93→class MergeScanOptions:
    94→    """Configuration bundle for merging a scan into persisted state."""
    95→
    96→    lang: str | None = None
    97→    scan_path: str | None = None
    98→    force_resolve: bool = False
    99→    exclude: tuple[str, ...] = ()
   100→    potentials: dict[str, int] | None = None
   101→    merge_potentials: bool = False
   102→    codebase_metrics: dict[str, Any] | None = None
   103→    include_slow: bool = True
   104→    ignore: list[str] | None = None
   105→    subjective_integrity_target: float | None = None
   106→
   107→
   108→def merge_scan(
   109→    state: StateModel,
   110→    current_findings: list[dict],
   111→    options: MergeScanOptions | None = None,
   112→) -> ScanDiff:
   113→    """Merge a fresh scan into existing state and return a diff summary."""
   114→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   115→    resolved_options = options or MergeScanOptions()
   116→
   117→    now = utc_now()
   118→    _record_scan_metadata(
   119→        state,
   120→        now,
   121→        lang=resolved_options.lang,
   122→        include_slow=resolved_options.include_slow,
   123→        scan_path=resolved_options.scan_path,
   124→    )
   125→    _merge_scan_inputs(
   126→        state,
   127→        lang=resolved_options.lang,
   128→        potentials=resolved_options.potentials,
   129→        merge_potentials=resolved_options.merge_potentials,
   130→        codebase_metrics=resolved_options.codebase_metrics,
   131→    )
   132→
   133→    existing = state["findings"]
   134→    ignore_patterns = (
   135→        resolved_options.ignore
   136→        if resolved_options.ignore is not None
   137→        else state.get("config", {}).get("ignore", [])
   138→    )
   139→    current_ids, new_count, reopened_count, current_by_detector, ignored_count, upsert_changed = (
   140→        upsert_findings(
   141→            existing,
   142→            current_findings,
   143→            ignore_patterns,
   144→            now,
   145→            lang=resolved_options.lang,
   146→        )
   147→    )
   148→
   149→    raw_findings = len(current_findings)
   150→    suppressed_pct = _compute_suppression(raw_findings, ignored_count)
   151→
   152→    ran_detectors = (
   153→        set(resolved_options.potentials.keys())
   154→        if resolved_options.potentials is not None
   155→        else None
   156→    )
   157→    suspect_detectors = find_suspect_detectors(
   158→        existing,
   159→        current_by_detector,
   160→        resolved_options.force_resolve,
   161→        ran_detectors,
   162→    )
   163→    auto_resolved, skipped_other_lang, skipped_out_of_scope, resolve_changed = auto_resolve_disappeared(
   164→        existing,
   165→        current_ids,
   166→        suspect_detectors,
   167→        now,
   168→        lang=resolved_options.lang,
   169→        scan_path=resolved_options.scan_path,
   170→        exclude=resolved_options.exclude,
   171→    )
   172→
   173→    # Mark subjective assessments stale when mechanical findings changed.
   174→    changed_detectors = upsert_changed | resolve_changed
   175→    if changed_detectors:
   176→        _mark_stale_on_mechanical_change(
   177→            state, changed_detectors=changed_detectors, now=now,
   178→        )
   179→
   180→    _recompute_stats(
   181→        state,
   182→        scan_path=resolved_options.scan_path,
   183→        subjective_integrity_target=resolved_options.subjective_integrity_target,
   184→    )
   185→    _append_scan_history(
   186→        state,
   187→        now=now,
   188→        lang=resolved_options.lang,
   189→        new_count=new_count,
   190→        auto_resolved=auto_resolved,
   191→        ignored_count=ignored_count,
   192→        raw_findings=raw_findings,
   193→        suppressed_pct=suppressed_pct,
   194→        ignore_pattern_count=len(ignore_patterns),
   195→    )
   196→
   197→    chronic_reopeners = [
   198→        finding
   199→        for finding in existing.values()
   200→        if finding.get("reopen_count", 0) >= 2 and finding["status"] == "open"
   201→    ]
   202→
   203→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   204→    return _build_merge_diff(
   205→        new_count=new_count,
   206→        auto_resolved=auto_resolved,
   207→        reopened_count=reopened_count,
   208→        current_ids=current_ids,
   209→        suspect_detectors=suspect_detectors,
   210→        chronic_reopeners=chronic_reopeners,
   211→        skipped_other_lang=skipped_other_lang,
   212→        skipped_out_of_scope=skipped_out_of_scope,
   213→        ignored_count=ignored_count,
   214→        ignore_pattern_count=len(ignore_patterns),
   215→        raw_findings=raw_findings,
   216→        suppressed_pct=suppressed_pct,
   217→    )
   218→

1→"""State persistence and migration routines."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→import logging
     7→import shutil
     8→import sys
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→from typing import cast
    11→
    12→__all__ = [
    13→    "load_state",
    14→    "save_state",
    15→]
    16→
    17→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import is_numeric
    18→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    19→    CURRENT_VERSION,
    20→    STATE_FILE,
    21→    StateModel,
    22→    empty_state,
    23→    ensure_state_defaults,
    24→    json_default,
    25→    validate_state_invariants,
    26→)
    27→from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats
    28→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import safe_write_text
    29→
    30→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    31→
    32→
    33→def _load_json(path: Path) -> dict[str, object]:
    34→    data = json.loads(path.read_text())
    35→    if not isinstance(data, dict):
    36→        raise ValueError("state file root must be a JSON object")
    37→    return data
    38→
    39→
    40→def _normalize_loaded_state(data: object) -> dict[str, object]:
    41→    if not isinstance(data, dict):
    42→        raise ValueError("state file root must be a JSON object")
    43→    ensure_state_defaults(data)
    44→    normalized = cast(StateModel, data)
    45→    validate_state_invariants(normalized)
    46→    return normalized
    47→
    48→
    49→def load_state(path: Path | None = None) -> StateModel:
    50→    """Load state from disk, or return empty state on missing/corruption."""
    51→    state_path = path or STATE_FILE
    52→    if not state_path.exists():
    53→        return empty_state()
    54→
    55→    try:
    56→        data = _load_json(state_path)
    57→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError, ValueError) as ex:
    58→        backup = state_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
    59→        if backup.exists():
    60→            try:
    61→                backup_data = _load_json(backup)
    62→                print(
    63→                    f"  ⚠ State file corrupted ({ex}), loaded from backup.",
    64→                    file=sys.stderr,
    65→                )
    66→                return _normalize_loaded_state(backup_data)
    67→            except (
    68→                json.JSONDecodeError,
    69→                UnicodeDecodeError,
    70→                OSError,
    71→                ValueError,
    72→                TypeError,
    73→                AttributeError,
    74→            ) as backup_ex:
    75→                logger.debug("Backup state load failed from %s: %s", backup, backup_ex)
    76→
    77→        print(f"  ⚠ State file corrupted ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    78→        rename_failed = False
    79→        try:
    80→            state_path.rename(state_path.with_suffix(".json.corrupted"))
    81→        except OSError as rename_ex:
    82→            rename_failed = True
    83→            logger.debug(
    84→                "Failed to rename corrupted state file %s: %s", state_path, rename_ex
    85→            )
    86→        if rename_failed:
    87→            logger.debug(
    88→                "Corrupted state file retained at original path: %s", state_path
    89→            )
    90→        return empty_state()
    91→
    92→    version = data.get("version", 1)
    93→    if version > CURRENT_VERSION:
    94→        print(
    95→            "  ⚠ State file version "
    96→            f"{version} is newer than supported ({CURRENT_VERSION}). "
    97→            "Some features may not work correctly.",
    98→            file=sys.stderr,
    99→        )
   100→
   101→    try:
   102→        return _normalize_loaded_state(data)
   103→    except (ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as normalize_ex:
   104→        print(
   105→            f"  ⚠ State invariants invalid ({normalize_ex}). Starting fresh.",
   106→            file=sys.stderr,
   107→        )
   108→        return empty_state()
   109→
   110→
   111→def _coerce_integrity_target(value: object) -> float | None:
   112→    if not is_numeric(value):
   113→        return None
   114→    return max(0.0, min(100.0, float(value)))
   115→
   116→
   117→def _resolve_integrity_target(
   118→    state: StateModel,
   119→    explicit_target: float | None,
   120→) -> float | None:
   121→    target = _coerce_integrity_target(explicit_target)
   122→    if target is not None:
   123→        return target
   124→
   125→    integrity = state.get("subjective_integrity")
   126→    if not isinstance(integrity, dict):
   127→        return None
   128→    return _coerce_integrity_target(integrity.get("target_score"))
   129→
   130→
   131→def save_state(
   132→    state: StateModel,
   133→    path: Path | None = None,
   134→    *,
   135→    subjective_integrity_target: float | None = None,
   136→) -> None:
   137→    """Recompute stats/score and save to disk atomically."""
   138→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   139→    _recompute_stats(
   140→        state,
   141→        scan_path=state.get("scan_path"),
   142→        subjective_integrity_target=_resolve_integrity_target(
   143→            state,
   144→            subjective_integrity_target,
   145→        ),
   146→    )
   147→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   148→
   149→    state_path = path or STATE_FILE
   150→    state_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   151→
   152→    content = json.dumps(state, indent=2, default=json_default) + "\n"
   153→
   154→    if state_path.exists():
   155→        backup = state_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
   156→        try:
   157→            shutil.copy2(str(state_path), str(backup))
   158→        except OSError as backup_ex:
   159→            logger.debug(
   160→                "Failed to create state backup %s: %s",
   161→                state_path.with_suffix(".json.bak"),
   162→                backup_ex,
   163→            )
   164→
   165→    try:
   166→        safe_write_text(state_path, content)
   167→    except OSError as ex:
   168→        print(f"  Warning: Could not save state: {ex}", file=sys.stderr)
   169→        raise
   170→

1→"""Finding upsert/auto-resolve helpers for scan merge."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._state.filtering import matched_ignore_pattern
     6→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import matches_exclusion
     7→
     8→
     9→def find_suspect_detectors(
    10→    existing: dict,
    11→    current_by_detector: dict[str, int],
    12→    force_resolve: bool,
    13→    ran_detectors: set[str] | None = None,
    14→) -> set[str]:
    15→    """Detectors that had open findings but likely did not actually run this scan."""
    16→    if force_resolve:
    17→        return set()
    18→
    19→    previous_open_by_detector: dict[str, int] = {}
    20→    for finding in existing.values():
    21→        if finding["status"] != "open":
    22→            continue
    23→        detector = finding.get("detector", "unknown")
    24→        previous_open_by_detector[detector] = (
    25→            previous_open_by_detector.get(detector, 0) + 1
    26→        )
    27→
    28→    # 'review' findings enter via `desloppify review --import`, not via scan phases.
    29→    # They are always marked suspect so the scan never auto-resolves them.
    30→    import_only_detectors = {"review"}
    31→    suspect: set[str] = set()
    32→
    33→    for detector, previous_count in previous_open_by_detector.items():
    34→        if detector in import_only_detectors:
    35→            suspect.add(detector)
    36→            continue
    37→        if current_by_detector.get(detector, 0) > 0:
    38→            continue
    39→        if ran_detectors is not None:
    40→            if detector not in ran_detectors:
    41→                suspect.add(detector)
    42→            continue
    43→        if previous_count >= 3:
    44→            suspect.add(detector)
    45→
    46→    return suspect
    47→
    48→
    49→def auto_resolve_disappeared(
    50→    existing: dict,
    51→    current_ids: set[str],
    52→    suspect_detectors: set[str],
    53→    now: str,
    54→    *,
    55→    lang: str | None,
    56→    scan_path: str | None,
    57→    exclude: tuple[str, ...] = (),
    58→) -> tuple[int, int, int, set[str]]:
    59→    """Auto-resolve open/wontfix/fixed/false_positive findings absent from scan.
    60→
    61→    Returns (resolved, skipped_other_lang, skipped_out_of_scope, resolved_detectors).
    62→    """
    63→    resolved = skipped_other_lang = skipped_out_of_scope = 0
    64→    resolved_detectors: set[str] = set()
    65→
    66→    for finding_id, previous in existing.items():
    67→        if finding_id in current_ids or previous["status"] not in (
    68→            "open",
    69→            "wontfix",
    70→            "fixed",
    71→            "false_positive",
    72→        ):
    73→            continue
    74→
    75→        if lang and previous.get("lang") and previous["lang"] != lang:
    76→            skipped_other_lang += 1
    77→            continue
    78→
    79→        if scan_path and scan_path != ".":
    80→            prefix = scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/"
    81→            if (
    82→                not previous["file"].startswith(prefix)
    83→                and previous["file"] != scan_path
    84→            ):
    85→                skipped_out_of_scope += 1
    86→                continue
    87→
    88→        if exclude and any(matches_exclusion(previous["file"], ex) for ex in exclude):
    89→            continue
    90→
    91→        if previous.get("detector", "unknown") in suspect_detectors:
    92→            continue
    93→
    94→        previous_status = previous["status"]
    95→        previous["status"] = "auto_resolved"
    96→        previous["resolved_at"] = now
    97→        previous["suppressed"] = False
    98→        previous["suppressed_at"] = None
    99→        previous["suppression_pattern"] = None
   100→        previous["resolution_attestation"] = {
   101→            "kind": "scan_verified",
   102→            "text": "Disappeared from detector output",
   103→            "attested_at": now,
   104→            "scan_verified": True,
   105→        }
   106→        previous["note"] = (
   107→            "Fixed despite wontfix — disappeared from scan (was wontfix)"
   108→            if previous_status == "wontfix"
   109→            else "Disappeared from scan — likely fixed"
   110→        )
   111→        resolved_detectors.add(previous.get("detector", "unknown"))
   112→        resolved += 1
   113→
   114→    return resolved, skipped_other_lang, skipped_out_of_scope, resolved_detectors
   115→
   116→
   117→def upsert_findings(
   118→    existing: dict,
   119→    current_findings: list[dict],
   120→    ignore: list[str],
   121→    now: str,
   122→    *,
   123→    lang: str | None,
   124→) -> tuple[set[str], int, int, dict[str, int], int, set[str]]:
   125→    """Insert new findings and update existing ones.
   126→
   127→    Returns (current_ids, new_count, reopened_count, by_detector, ignored_count, changed_detectors).
   128→    """
   129→    current_ids: set[str] = set()
   130→    new_count = reopened_count = ignored_count = 0
   131→    by_detector: dict[str, int] = {}
   132→    changed_detectors: set[str] = set()
   133→
   134→    for finding in current_findings:
   135→        finding_id = finding["id"]
   136→        detector = finding.get("detector", "unknown")
   137→        current_ids.add(finding_id)
   138→        by_detector[detector] = by_detector.get(detector, 0) + 1
   139→        matched_ignore = matched_ignore_pattern(finding_id, finding["file"], ignore)
   140→        if matched_ignore:
   141→            ignored_count += 1
   142→
   143→        if lang:
   144→            finding["lang"] = lang
   145→
   146→        if finding_id not in existing:
   147→            existing[finding_id] = dict(finding)
   148→            if matched_ignore:
   149→                existing[finding_id]["suppressed"] = True
   150→                existing[finding_id]["suppressed_at"] = now
   151→                existing[finding_id]["suppression_pattern"] = matched_ignore
   152→                continue
   153→            new_count += 1
   154→            changed_detectors.add(detector)
   155→            continue
   156→
   157→        previous = existing[finding_id]
   158→        previous.update(
   159→            last_seen=now,
   160→            tier=finding["tier"],
   161→            confidence=finding["confidence"],
   162→            summary=finding["summary"],
   163→            detail=finding.get("detail", {}),
   164→        )
   165→        if "zone" in finding:
   166→            previous["zone"] = finding["zone"]
   167→        if lang and not previous.get("lang"):
   168→            previous["lang"] = lang
   169→
   170→        if matched_ignore:
   171→            previous["suppressed"] = True
   172→            previous["suppressed_at"] = now
   173→            previous["suppression_pattern"] = matched_ignore
   174→            if previous["status"] in ("fixed", "auto_resolved", "false_positive"):
   175→                previous["status"] = "open"
   176→                previous["resolved_at"] = None
   177→                previous["note"] = (
   178→                    "Suppressed by ignore pattern — remains unresolved for score integrity"
   179→                )
   180→            continue
   181→
   182→        previous["suppressed"] = False
   183→        previous["suppressed_at"] = None
   184→        previous["suppression_pattern"] = None
   185→
   186→        if previous["status"] in ("fixed", "auto_resolved"):
   187→            # subjective_review findings are condition-based.  When just
   188→            # auto-resolved by an agent import, skip reopening to avoid a
   189→            # resolve-then-reopen loop on the same scan cycle.
   190→            if (
   191→                detector == "subjective_review"
   192→                and previous["status"] == "auto_resolved"
   193→                and (previous.get("resolution_attestation") or {}).get("kind") == "agent_import"
   194→            ):
   195→                continue
   196→            previous_status = previous["status"]
   197→            previous["reopen_count"] = previous.get("reopen_count", 0) + 1
   198→            previous.pop("resolution_attestation", None)
   199→            previous.update(
   200→                status="open",
   201→                resolved_at=None,
   202→                note=(
   203→                    f"Reopened (×{previous['reopen_count']}) "
   204→                    f"— reappeared in scan (was {previous_status})"
   205→                ),
   206→            )
   207→            reopened_count += 1
   208→            changed_detectors.add(detector)
   209→
   210→    return current_ids, new_count, reopened_count, by_detector, ignored_count, changed_detectors
   211→
   212→
   213→__all__ = [
   214→    "auto_resolve_disappeared",
   215→    "find_suspect_detectors",
   216→    "upsert_findings",
   217→]
   218→

1→"""Tests for desloppify.scoring — objective dimension-based scoring system."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import pytest
     6→
     7→from desloppify.scoring import (
     8→    CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS,
     9→    DIMENSIONS,
    10→    DISPLAY_NAMES,
    11→    MIN_SAMPLE,
    12→    SUBJECTIVE_CHECKS,
    13→    TIER_WEIGHTS,
    14→    Dimension,
    15→    compute_dimension_scores,
    16→    compute_health_breakdown,
    17→    compute_health_score,
    18→    compute_score_bundle,
    19→    compute_score_impact,
    20→    detector_pass_rate,
    21→    get_dimension_for_detector,
    22→    merge_potentials,
    23→)
    24→
    25→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    26→# Helpers
    27→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    28→
    29→
    30→def _finding(
    31→    detector: str,
    32→    *,
    33→    status: str = "open",
    34→    confidence: str = "high",
    35→    file: str = "a.py",
    36→    zone: str = "production",
    37→) -> dict:
    38→    """Build a minimal finding dict."""
    39→    return {
    40→        "detector": detector,
    41→        "status": status,
    42→        "confidence": confidence,
    43→        "file": file,
    44→        "zone": zone,
    45→    }
    46→
    47→
    48→def _findings_dict(*findings: dict) -> dict:
    49→    """Wrap a list of finding dicts into an id-keyed dict."""
    50→    return {str(i): f for i, f in enumerate(findings)}
    51→

    32→  status                        Score dashboard with per-tier progress
    33→  tree                          Annotated codebase tree (zoom with --focus)
    34→  show <pattern>                Dig into findings by file/dir/detector/ID

    47→examples:

    56→  desloppify resolve fixed "logs::src/foo.tsx::*" --note "removed debug logs" --attest "I have actually removed all console.log calls from foo.tsx and I am not gaming the score by resolving without fixing."
    57→  desloppify resolve wontfix deprecated --note "migration in progress" --attest "I have actually verified these are tracked in the migration plan and I am not gaming the score by resolving without fixing."
    58→  desloppify ignore "smells::*::async_no_await" --attest "I have actually verified these are intentional fire-and-forget patterns and I am not gaming the score by resolving without fixing."
    59→  desloppify detect logs --top 10

    61→  desloppify review --validate-import findings.json --attested-external --attest "I validated this review was completed without awareness of overall score and is unbiased."
    62→  desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude
    63→  desloppify review --external-submit --session-id <session_id> --import findings.json
    64→  desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import --retrospective
    65→  desloppify dev scaffold-lang go --extension .go --marker go.mod --default-src .
    66→  desloppify move src/shared/hooks/useFoo.ts src/shared/hooks/video/useFoo.ts --dry-run
    67→  desloppify move scripts/foo/bar.py scripts/foo/baz/bar.py
    68→"""
    69→
    70→
    71→class _NoAbbrevArgumentParser(argparse.ArgumentParser):
    72→    """Argparse parser variant that disables long-option abbreviation."""
    73→
    74→    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
    75→        kwargs.setdefault("allow_abbrev", False)
    76→        super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
    77→
    78→
    79→def _cli_version_string() -> str:
    80→    """Return the best available CLI version label."""
    81→    try:
    82→        return f"desloppify {get_version('desloppify')}"
    83→    except PackageNotFoundError:
    84→        return "desloppify (version unknown)"
    85→
    86→
    87→def create_parser(*, langs: list[str], detector_names: list[str]) -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
    88→    """Build top-level CLI parser with all subcommands."""
    89→    lang_help = ", ".join(langs) if langs else "registered languages"
    90→
    91→    parser = _NoAbbrevArgumentParser(
    92→        prog="desloppify",
    93→        description="Desloppify — codebase health tracker",
    94→        epilog=USAGE_EXAMPLES,
    95→        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
    96→    )
    97→    parser.add_argument(
    98→        "--lang",
    99→        type=str,
   100→        default=None,
   101→        help=f"Language to scan ({lang_help}). Auto-detected if omitted.",
   102→    )
   103→    parser.add_argument(
   104→        "--exclude",
   105→        action="append",
   106→        default=None,
   107→        metavar="PATTERN",
   108→        help="Path pattern to exclude (component/prefix match; repeatable)",
   109→    )
   110→    parser.add_argument(
   111→        "--version",
   112→        "-V",
   113→        action="version",
   114→        version=_cli_version_string(),
   115→    )
   116→    sub = parser.add_subparsers(
   117→        dest="command",
   118→        required=True,
   119→        parser_class=_NoAbbrevArgumentParser,
   120→    )
   121→    _add_scan_parser(sub)
   122→    _add_status_parser(sub)
   123→    _add_tree_parser(sub)
   124→    _add_show_parser(sub)
   125→    _add_next_parser(sub)
   126→    _add_resolve_parser(sub)
   127→    _add_ignore_parser(sub)
   128→    _add_fix_parser(sub, langs)
   129→    _add_plan_parser(sub)
   130→    _add_viz_parser(sub)
   131→    _add_detect_parser(sub, detector_names)
   132→    _add_move_parser(sub)
   133→    _add_review_parser(sub)
   134→    _add_zone_parser(sub)
   135→    _add_config_parser(sub)
   136→    _add_dev_parser(sub)
   137→    _add_langs_parser(sub)
   138→    _add_update_skill_parser(sub)
   139→    return parser
   140→
   141→
   142→__all__ = ["USAGE_EXAMPLES", "create_parser"]

    24→def coerce_assessment_score(value: object) -> float | None:
    25→    """Normalize a subjective assessment score payload to a 0-100 float.
    26→
    27→    Returns ``None`` when the value cannot be interpreted as a numeric score
    28→    (e.g. bools, non-numeric strings, missing keys).
    29→    """
    30→    if is_numeric(value):
    31→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(value))), 1)
    32→    if isinstance(value, dict):
    33→        raw = value.get("score")
    34→        if not is_numeric(raw):
    35→            return None
    36→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(raw))), 1)
    37→    return None
    38→

1→"""config command: show/set/unset project configuration."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→import sys
     7→
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
     9→from desloppify.core.config import (
    10→    CONFIG_SCHEMA,
    11→    save_config,
    12→    set_config_value,
    13→    unset_config_value,
    14→)
    15→from desloppify.core.fallbacks import print_error
    16→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    17→
    18→
    19→def cmd_config(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    20→    """Handle config subcommands: show, set, unset."""
    21→    action = getattr(args, "config_action", None)
    22→    if action == "set":
    23→        _config_set(args)
    24→    elif action == "unset":
    25→        _config_unset(args)
    26→    else:
    27→        _config_show(args)
    28→
    29→
    30→def _config_show(args):
    31→    """Print all config keys with current values and descriptions."""
    32→    config = command_runtime(args).config
    33→
    34→    print(colorize("\n  Desloppify Configuration\n", "bold"))
    35→    for key, schema in CONFIG_SCHEMA.items():
    36→        value = config.get(key, schema.default)
    37→        is_default = value == schema.default
    38→
    39→        # Format display value
    40→        if schema.type is int and key.endswith("_days") and value == 0:
    41→            display = "never (0)"
    42→        elif isinstance(value, list):
    43→            display = ", ".join(value) if value else "(empty)"
    44→        elif isinstance(value, dict):
    45→            display = f"{len(value)} entries" if value else "(empty)"
    46→        else:
    47→            display = str(value)
    48→
    49→        default_tag = colorize(" (default)", "dim") if is_default else ""
    50→        print(f"  {key:<25} {display}{default_tag}")
    51→        print(colorize(f"  {'':25} {schema.description}", "dim"))
    52→    print()
    53→
    54→
    55→def _config_set(args):
    56→    """Set a config key to a value."""
    57→    config = command_runtime(args).config
    58→    key = args.config_key
    59→    value = args.config_value
    60→
    61→    try:
    62→        set_config_value(config, key, value)
    63→    except (KeyError, ValueError) as e:
    64→        print_error(str(e))
    65→        sys.exit(1)
    66→
    67→    try:
    68→        save_config(config)
    69→    except OSError as e:
    70→        print_error(f"could not save config: {e}")
    71→        sys.exit(1)
    72→    display = config[key]
    73→    if isinstance(display, int) and key.endswith("_days") and display == 0:
    74→        display = "never (0)"
    75→    print(colorize(f"  Set {key} = {display}", "green"))
    76→
    77→
    78→def _config_unset(args):
    79→    """Reset a config key to its default."""
    80→    config = command_runtime(args).config
    81→    key = args.config_key
    82→
    83→    try:
    84→        unset_config_value(config, key)
    85→    except KeyError as e:
    86→        print_error(str(e))
    87→        sys.exit(1)
    88→
    89→    try:
    90→        save_config(config)
    91→    except OSError as e:
    92→        print_error(f"could not save config: {e}")
    93→        sys.exit(1)
    94→    default = CONFIG_SCHEMA[key].default
    95→    print(colorize(f"  Reset {key} to default ({default})", "green"))
    96→

    41→
    42→def cmd_status(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    43→    """Show score dashboard."""
    44→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    45→    state = runtime.state
    46→    config = runtime.config
    47→
    48→    stats = state.get("stats", {})
    49→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {}) or {}
    50→    scorecard_dims = scorecard_dimensions_payload(state, dim_scores=dim_scores)
    51→    subjective_measures = [row for row in scorecard_dims if row.get("subjective")]
    52→    suppression = state_mod.suppression_metrics(state)
    53→
    54→    if getattr(args, "json", False):
    55→        print(
    56→            json.dumps(
    57→                _status_json_payload(
    58→                    state,
    59→                    stats,
    60→                    dim_scores,
    61→                    scorecard_dims,
    62→                    subjective_measures,
    63→                    suppression,
    64→                ),
    65→                indent=2,
    66→            )
    67→        )
    68→        return
    69→
    70→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    71→        return
    72→
    73→    stale_warning = check_tool_staleness(state)
    74→    if stale_warning:
    75→        print(colorize(f"  {stale_warning}", "yellow"))
    76→    skill_warning = check_skill_version()
    77→    if skill_warning:
    78→        print(colorize(f"  {skill_warning}", "yellow"))
    79→
    80→    scores = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    81→    by_tier = stats.get("by_tier", {})
    82→    target_strict_score = target_strict_score_from_config(config, fallback=95.0)
    83→
    84→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
    85→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
    86→
    87→    # Load living plan for plan-aware rendering and narrative
    88→    _plan = load_plan()
    89→    _plan_active = _plan if (
    90→        _plan.get("queue_order") or _plan.get("clusters")
    91→    ) else None
    92→
    93→    narrative = compute_narrative(
    94→        state,
    95→        context=NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="status", plan=_plan_active),
    96→    )
    97→    ignores = config.get("ignore", [])
    98→
    99→    for line, style in score_summary_lines(
   100→        overall_score=scores.overall,
   101→        objective_score=scores.objective,
   102→        strict_score=scores.strict,
   103→        verified_strict_score=scores.verified,
   104→    ):
   105→        print(colorize(line, style))
   106→    print_scan_metrics(state)
   107→    print_open_scope_breakdown(state)
   108→    print_scan_completeness(state)
   109→
   110→    if dim_scores:
   111→        show_dimension_table(state, dim_scores)
   112→        reporting_dimensions_mod.show_score_model_breakdown(
   113→            state,
   114→            dim_scores=dim_scores,
   115→        )
   116→    else:
   117→        show_tier_progress_table(by_tier)
   118→
   119→    if dim_scores:
   120→        show_focus_suggestion(dim_scores, state, plan=_plan_active)
   121→        show_subjective_followup(
   122→            state,
   123→            dim_scores,
   124→            target_strict_score=target_strict_score,
   125→        )
   126→
   127→    show_review_summary(state)
   128→    show_structural_areas(state)
   129→    show_agent_plan(narrative, plan=_plan_active)
   130→
   131→    if narrative.get("headline"):
   132→        print(colorize(f"  -> {narrative['headline']}", "cyan"))
   133→        print()
   134→
   135→    if ignores:
   136→        show_ignore_summary(ignores, suppression)
   137→
   138→    review_age = config.get("review_max_age_days", 30)
   139→    if review_age != 30:
   140→        label = "never" if review_age == 0 else f"{review_age} days"
   141→        print(colorize(f"  Review staleness: {label}", "dim"))
   142→    print()
   143→
   144→    write_status_query(
   145→        state=state,
   146→        stats=stats,
   147→        by_tier=by_tier,
   148→        dim_scores=dim_scores,
   149→        scorecard_dims=scorecard_dims,
   150→        subjective_measures=subjective_measures,

   154→        overall_score=scores.overall,

   160→
   161→
   162→def _status_json_payload(
   163→    state: dict,
   164→    stats: dict,
   165→    dim_scores: dict,
   166→    scorecard_dims: list[dict],
   167→    subjective_measures: list[dict],
   168→    suppression: dict,
   169→) -> dict:
   170→    scores = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
   171→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
   172→    open_scope = (
   173→        state_mod.open_scope_breakdown(findings, state.get("scan_path"))
   174→        if isinstance(findings, dict)
   175→        else None
   176→    )

1→"""State-path and scan-gating helpers for command modules."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import auto_detect_lang_name
     8→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import PROJECT_ROOT
     9→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    10→
    11→
    12→def _sole_existing_lang_state_file() -> Path | None:
    13→    """Return the only existing language-specific state file, if unambiguous."""
    14→    state_dir = PROJECT_ROOT / ".desloppify"
    15→    if not state_dir.exists():
    16→        return None
    17→    candidates = sorted(path for path in state_dir.glob("state-*.json") if path.is_file())
    18→    if len(candidates) == 1:
    19→        return candidates[0]
    20→    return None
    21→
    22→
    23→def _allow_lang_state_fallback(args) -> bool:
    24→    """Whether command can safely fallback to the sole existing lang state file."""
    25→    # Scan should always honor detected/explicit language mapping to avoid cross-lang merges.
    26→    return getattr(args, "command", None) != "scan"
    27→
    28→
    29→def state_path(args) -> Path | None:
    30→    """Get state file path from args, or None for default."""
    31→    path_arg = getattr(args, "state", None)
    32→    if path_arg:
    33→        return Path(path_arg)
    34→    lang_name = getattr(args, "lang", None)
    35→    if not lang_name:
    36→        lang_name = auto_detect_lang_name(args)
    37→    if lang_name:
    38→        resolved = PROJECT_ROOT / ".desloppify" / f"state-{lang_name}.json"
    39→        if resolved.exists() or not _allow_lang_state_fallback(args):
    40→            return resolved
    41→        fallback = _sole_existing_lang_state_file()
    42→        if fallback is not None:
    43→            return fallback
    44→        return resolved
    45→
    46→    if _allow_lang_state_fallback(args):
    47→        fallback = _sole_existing_lang_state_file()
    48→        if fallback is not None:
    49→            return fallback
    50→    return None
    51→
    52→
    53→def require_completed_scan(state: dict) -> bool:
    54→    """Return True when the state contains at least one completed scan."""
    55→    has_completed_scan = bool(state.get("last_scan"))
    56→    if not has_completed_scan:
    57→        print(colorize("No scans yet. Run: desloppify scan", "yellow"))
    58→    return has_completed_scan
    59→

1→"""Runtime context helpers for command handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→from typing import Any
     8→
     9→from desloppify import state as state_mod
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
    11→from desloppify.core.config import load_config
    12→
    13→
    14→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    15→class CommandRuntime:
    16→    """Explicit runtime dependencies shared by command handlers."""
    17→
    18→    config: dict[str, Any]
    19→    state: dict[str, Any]
    20→    state_path: Path | None
    21→
    22→
    23→def command_runtime(args) -> CommandRuntime:
    24→    """Return runtime context from explicit args.runtime or construct one."""
    25→    runtime = getattr(args, "runtime", None)
    26→    if isinstance(runtime, CommandRuntime):
    27→        return runtime
    28→
    29→    config = load_config()
    30→    state_file = state_path(args)
    31→    if isinstance(state_file, str):
    32→        state_file = Path(state_file)
    33→
    34→    state = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
    35→
    36→    return CommandRuntime(config=config, state=state, state_path=state_file)
    37→
    38→
    39→__all__ = ["CommandRuntime", "command_runtime"]
    40→

1→"""Tool metadata helpers (hashing, staleness checks)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import hashlib
     6→import logging
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify.core.fallbacks import log_best_effort_failure
    10→
    11→TOOL_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    12→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    13→
    14→
    15→def _compute_tool_hash_with_diagnostics(
    16→    *,
    17→    tool_dir: Path | None = None,
    18→) -> tuple[str, int]:
    19→    """Compute tool hash and unreadable-file count."""
    20→    active_tool_dir = tool_dir or TOOL_DIR
    21→    digest = hashlib.sha256()
    22→    unreadable_files = 0
    23→    for py_file in sorted(active_tool_dir.rglob("*.py")):
    24→        rel_parts = py_file.relative_to(active_tool_dir).parts
    25→        if "tests" in rel_parts:
    26→            continue
    27→        try:
    28→            digest.update(str(py_file.relative_to(active_tool_dir)).encode())
    29→            digest.update(py_file.read_bytes())
    30→        except OSError as exc:
    31→            unreadable_files += 1
    32→            log_best_effort_failure(
    33→                logger,
    34→                f"read tool-hash source file {py_file}",
    35→                exc,
    36→            )
    37→            digest.update(f"[unreadable:{py_file.name}]".encode())
    38→            continue
    39→    return digest.hexdigest()[:12], unreadable_files
    40→
    41→
    42→def compute_tool_hash(*, tool_dir: Path | None = None) -> str:
    43→    """Compute a content hash of all .py files in the desloppify package."""
    44→    digest, _ = _compute_tool_hash_with_diagnostics(tool_dir=tool_dir)
    45→    return digest
    46→
    47→
    48→def check_tool_staleness(state: dict, *, tool_dir: Path | None = None) -> str | None:
    49→    """Return warning if tool code has changed since last scan."""
    50→    stored = state.get("tool_hash")
    51→    if not stored:
    52→        return None

    54→    if current != stored:
    55→        suffix = (
    56→            f" ({unreadable_files} unreadable file(s) encountered while hashing)"
    57→            if unreadable_files > 0
    58→            else ""
    59→        )
    60→        return (
    61→            f"Tool code changed since last scan (was {stored}, now {current}){suffix}. "
    62→            "Consider re-running: desloppify scan"
    63→        )
    64→    if unreadable_files > 0:
    65→        return (
    66→            f"Tool hash check completed with {unreadable_files} unreadable file(s); "
    67→            "staleness verification may be incomplete."
    68→        )
    69→    return None
    70→
    71→
    72→__all__ = ["TOOL_DIR", "check_tool_staleness", "compute_tool_hash"]

## Implementation Plan: Fix Ignore/Exclude Score Corruption (Issue #168)

### Overview of the Bug

The bug has two root causes operating in tandem:

1. **The reopen hack** in `filtering.py:121-126` and `merge_findings.py:174-180`: When a finding matches an ignore pattern, the code reopens resolved findings (changing `fixed`/`auto_resolved`/`false_positive` to `open`) under the belief that this preserves "score integrity." This is the original corruption vector -- it undoes legitimate work.

2. **Suppressed findings are not filtered from scoring**: Both `_count_findings()` in `scoring.py:38-50` and `_iter_scoring_candidates()` in `detection.py:35-46` iterate over all findings without checking `finding.get("suppressed")`. This means suppressed findings still count as open debt, dragging scores down.

The combination is devastating: resolved findings get reopened to `status="open"`, then still count in scoring because suppression is not checked. Score collapses.

---

### Step 1: Filter suppressed findings from scoring (the core fix)

**Rationale**: Suppressed findings must be invisible to all scoring pathways. There are three filter points that need modification.

#### 1a. `_count_findings()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/scoring.py` (line 38-50)

Add a suppression filter. Currently:

```python
def _count_findings(findings: dict) -> tuple[dict[str, int], dict[int, dict[str, int]]]:
    counters = dict.fromkeys(_EMPTY_COUNTERS, 0)
    tier_stats: dict[int, dict[str, int]] = {}
    for finding in findings.values():
        status = finding["status"]
        ...
```

Change to skip suppressed findings:

```python
    for finding in findings.values():
        if finding.get("suppressed"):
            continue
        status = finding["status"]
        ...
```

This is the stats counter used for the dashboard. Suppressed findings should not appear in any status count.

#### 1b. `_iter_scoring_candidates()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_scoring/detection.py` (line 35-46)

Add suppression filter. Currently yields any finding matching detector + zone. Change to:

```python
def _iter_scoring_candidates(
    detector: str,
    findings: dict[str, Finding],
    excluded_zones: frozenset[str],
):
    for finding in findings.values():
        if finding.get("suppressed"):
            continue
        if finding.get("detector") != detector:
            continue
        if finding.get("zone", "production") in excluded_zones:
            continue
        yield finding
```

This is the critical scoring path -- all dimension scores flow through here.

#### 1c. `open_scope_breakdown()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py` (line 52-77)

Add suppression filter in the loop. Currently checks `status != "open"` but not `suppressed`. Add:

```python
    for finding in findings.values():
        if finding.get("suppressed"):
            continue
        if finding.get("status") != "open":
            continue
```

This function is used by `status_cmd` and `scan_artifacts` for the in-scope/out-of-scope breakdown display.

---

### Step 2: Remove the problematic reopen-to-open hack

**Rationale**: The reopening of resolved findings when they match an ignore pattern was a misguided attempt to prevent score gaming. The attestation requirement already handles this -- if someone ignores resolved findings, the attestation proves intent. Reopening them actually *creates* the corruption because combined with the non-filtering of suppressed findings from scoring, it creates phantom open debt.

#### 2a. `remove_ignored_findings()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py` (line 107-132)

Remove lines 121-126 entirely. The function should just set `suppressed=True` and related fields, without touching `status`, `resolved_at`, or `note`. The corrected version:

```python
def remove_ignored_findings(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
    ensure_state_defaults(state)
    matched_ids = [
        finding_id
        for finding_id, finding in state["findings"].items()
        if is_ignored(finding_id, finding["file"], [pattern])
    ]
    now = utc_now()
    for finding_id in matched_ids:
        finding = state["findings"][finding_id]
        finding["suppressed"] = True
        finding["suppressed_at"] = now
        finding["suppression_pattern"] = pattern
    # (removed: the block that reopened fixed/auto_resolved/false_positive findings)
    from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats
    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
    validate_state_invariants(state)
    return len(matched_ids)
```

#### 2b. `upsert_findings()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/merge_findings.py` (line 170-180)

The same reopen logic exists here for the scan-time path. Currently lines 174-179:

```python
        if matched_ignore:
            previous["suppressed"] = True
            previous["suppressed_at"] = now
            previous["suppression_pattern"] = matched_ignore
            if previous["status"] in ("fixed", "auto_resolved", "false_positive"):
                previous["status"] = "open"
                previous["resolved_at"] = None
                previous["note"] = (
                    "Suppressed by ignore pattern -- remains unresolved for score integrity"
                )
            continue
```

Remove the `if previous["status"] in ...` block. Just set suppressed fields and continue:

```python
        if matched_ignore:
            previous["suppressed"] = True
            previous["suppressed_at"] = now
            previous["suppression_pattern"] = matched_ignore
            continue
```

---

### Step 3: Add `needs_rescan` state flag

**Rationale**: When ignore or exclude config changes, the current scores are based on pre-change state. Findings were suppressed/removed in-memory, but a proper rescan is needed to get accurate potentials and to discover any new findings in previously excluded paths.

#### 3a. Add config-level flag in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/config.py`

Add to `CONFIG_SCHEMA`:

```python
"needs_rescan": ConfigKey(
    bool, False, "Set when config changes require a rescan for accurate scores"
),
```

**Why config, not state?**: The flag relates to configuration changes (ignore/exclude patterns) that make the persisted state potentially inaccurate. Putting it in config means it persists across state loads and is cleared by the scan command which reads config. Using config also means it is language-independent (a config change affects all languages).

#### 3b. Set the flag in ignore command

In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py`, in `cmd_ignore_pattern()` after the config save (around line 169):

```python
config["needs_rescan"] = True
_save_config_or_exit(config)
```

Also print a prompt:

```python

```

#### 3c. Set the flag in the new exclude command (see Step 4)

Same pattern: after adding exclude pattern, set `config["needs_rescan"] = True`.

#### 3d. Clear the flag after scan

In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_workflow.py`, in `merge_scan_results()` after the state save succeeds (around line 415):

```python

    runtime.config["needs_rescan"] = False
    try:
        from desloppify.core.config import save_config
        save_config(runtime.config)
    except OSError:
        pass  # Best-effort
```

### Step 4: Add `exclude` CLI subcommand

**Rationale**: Currently the only way to add persistent exclude patterns is to manually edit `config.json` or use `desloppify config set exclude <pattern>`. A dedicated `exclude` command parallels the existing `ignore` command.

#### 4a. Add parser in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups.py`

Add after `_add_ignore_parser`:

```python
def _add_exclude_parser(sub) -> None:
    p_exclude = sub.add_parser(
        "exclude", help="Add path pattern to exclude list, requires rescan"
    )
    p_exclude.add_argument("pattern", help="Path pattern to exclude from scanning")
    p_exclude.add_argument("--state", type=str, default=None)
```

Add `_add_exclude_parser` to the `__all__` list.

#### 4b. Register parser in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/cli_support/parser.py`

Import and call `_add_exclude_parser(sub)` in `create_parser()`.

#### 4c. Add config helper in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/config.py`

```python
def add_exclude_pattern(config: dict, pattern: str) -> None:
    """Append a pattern to the exclude list (deduplicates)."""
    excludes = config.setdefault("exclude", [])
    if pattern not in excludes:
        excludes.append(pattern)
```

#### 4d. Create command handler

New file: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/exclude_cmd.py`

```python
"""exclude command: add path pattern to exclude list."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import sys

from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
from desloppify.core import config as config_mod
from desloppify.core.fallbacks import print_error
from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize


def cmd_exclude(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Add a path pattern to the exclude list."""
    config = command_runtime(args).config
    pattern = args.pattern

    if pattern in config.get("exclude", []):
        print(colorize(f"  Pattern already excluded: {pattern}", "yellow"))
        return

    config_mod.add_exclude_pattern(config, pattern)
    config["needs_rescan"] = True

    try:
        config_mod.save_config(config)
    except OSError as exc:
        print_error(f"could not save config: {exc}")
        sys.exit(1)

    print(colorize(f"  Added exclude pattern: {pattern}", "green"))
    print(colorize(
        "  Scores may be stale. Run: desloppify scan",
        "yellow",
    ))
```

#### 4e. Register in command registry

In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/registry.py`, import `cmd_exclude` and add `"exclude": cmd_exclude` to the handler dict.

#### 4f. Update usage examples

In `parser.py`'s `USAGE_EXAMPLES`, add:
```
  exclude <pattern>             Add path pattern to exclude from scanning
```

### Step 5: Warning on all commands when `needs_rescan` is set

**Rationale**: Every command that reads state should warn the user that scores are potentially stale after ignore/exclude changes.

#### 5a. Create a centralized check function

Add to `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py`:

```python
def check_needs_rescan(config: dict) -> str | None:
    """Return warning message if config changes require a rescan."""
    if config.get("needs_rescan"):
        return (
            "Config changed since last scan (ignore/exclude patterns updated). "
            "Scores may be stale. Run: desloppify scan"
        )
    return None
```

#### 6a. New test file: `desloppify/tests/state/test_suppression_scoring.py`

**Test: suppressed findings excluded from `_count_findings`**
```python
def test_count_findings_excludes_suppressed():
    findings = {
        "f1": {"status": "open", "tier": 2, "suppressed": False},
        "f2": {"status": "open", "tier": 2, "suppressed": True},
        "f3": {"status": "fixed", "tier": 3, "suppressed": True},
    }
    counters, by_tier = scoring_mod._count_findings(findings)
    assert counters["open"] == 1  # Only f1
    assert counters["fixed"] == 0  # f3 is suppressed
    assert 2 in by_tier
    assert by_tier[2]["open"] == 1
```

**Test: suppressed findings excluded from `_iter_scoring_candidates`**

def test_iter_scoring_candidates_skips_suppressed():
    findings = {
        "f1": {"detector": "unused", "zone": "production", "suppressed": False},
        "f2": {"detector": "unused", "zone": "production", "suppressed": True},
    }
    results = list(detection_mod._iter_scoring_candidates("unused", findings, frozenset()))
    assert len(results) == 1
    assert results[0] is findings["f1"]
```

        "f2": {"status": "open", "file": "b.py", "suppressed": True},

def test_remove_ignored_findings_preserves_resolved_status():
    state = schema_mod.empty_state()
    state["findings"]["f1"] = {
        "id": "f1", "detector": "unused", "file": "a.py",
        "tier": 2, "confidence": "high", "summary": "test",
        "detail": {}, "status": "fixed", "note": None,
        "first_seen": "2025-01-01", "last_seen": "2025-01-01",
        "resolved_at": "2025-01-02", "reopen_count": 0,
    }
    state["potentials"] = {"python": {"unused": 10}}
    
    count = filtering_mod.remove_ignored_findings(state, "a.py")
    assert count == 1
    assert state["findings"]["f1"]["status"] == "fixed"  # NOT reopened
    assert state["findings"]["f1"]["suppressed"] is True
    assert state["findings"]["f1"]["resolved_at"] == "2025-01-02"  # preserved
```

### Implementation Order and Dependencies

1. **Step 1 (scoring filter)** and **Step 2 (remove reopen hack)** should be done together as one atomic change -- they are the core bug fix and are logically inseparable.

2. **Step 3 (needs_rescan flag)** depends on Step 1+2 being done (the flag makes sense only when the suppression fix is in place).

3. **Step 4 (exclude command)** can be done independently but should come after Step 3 since it uses the `needs_rescan` flag.

4. **Step 5 (warnings)** depends on Step 3.

5. **Step 6 (tests)** should be written alongside each step, but the regression tests for the core bug (6a-6c) should go in with Step 1+2.

### Risk Assessment

- **Low risk**: Steps 1a, 1b, 1c are additive filters. They only narrow what gets counted. If any finding is incorrectly marked as `suppressed=True` that is a pre-existing data issue, not caused by this change.

- **Medium risk**: Step 2 (removing the reopen hack) changes behavior. Any state files that already have corrupted `status="open"` + `suppressed=True` findings from the old behavior will need the reopen reversed. Consider adding a one-time migration: any finding with `suppressed=True` AND `status="open"` AND `note` containing "Suppressed by ignore pattern" should have its status restored. However, we cannot know the original status. The pragmatic approach is: let the next scan auto-resolve them (since they won't appear in detector output), and the suppression filter (Step 1) ensures they do not affect scoring in the meantime.

- **Low risk**: Steps 3-5 are config/UI changes with no impact on scoring logic.

### Critical Files for Implementation

- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/scoring.py` - Core fix: add suppression filter to `_count_findings()`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_scoring/detection.py` - Core fix: add suppression filter to `_iter_scoring_candidates()`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py` - Remove reopen hack in `remove_ignored_findings()` + filter `open_scope_breakdown()`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/merge_findings.py` - Remove reopen hack in `upsert_findings()`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/config.py` - Add `needs_rescan` config key + `add_exclude_pattern()` helper
